WCS[#](#wcs "Link to this heading")
===================================

*class* astropy.wcs.WCS(*header=None*, *fobj=None*, *key=' '*, *minerr=0.0*, *relax=True*, *naxis=None*, *keysel=None*, *colsel=None*, *fix=True*, *translate\_units=''*, *\_do\_set=True*, *preserve\_units=False*)[[source]](../_modules/astropy/wcs/wcs.html#WCS)[#](#astropy.wcs.WCS "Link to this definition")
:   Bases: `FITSWCSAPIMixin`, [`WCSBase`](astropy.wcs.WCSBase.html#astropy.wcs.WCSBase "astropy.wcs.WCSBase")

    WCS objects perform standard WCS transformations, and correct for
    [SIP](https://irsa.ipac.caltech.edu/data/SPITZER/docs/files/spitzer/shupeADASS.pdf) and [distortion paper](https://www.atnf.csiro.au/people/mcalabre/WCS/dcs_20040422.pdf) table-lookup transformations, based
    on the WCS keywords and supplementary data read from a FITS file.

    See also: <https://docs.astropy.org/en/stable/wcs/>

    Parameters:
    :   **header**[`Header`](../io/fits/api/headers.html#astropy.io.fits.Header "astropy.io.fits.Header"), [`PrimaryHDU`](../io/fits/api/hdus.html#astropy.io.fits.PrimaryHDU "astropy.io.fits.hdu.image.PrimaryHDU"), [`ImageHDU`](../io/fits/api/images.html#astropy.io.fits.ImageHDU "astropy.io.fits.hdu.image.ImageHDU"), [`str`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/stdtypes.html#str "(in Python v3.15)"), dict-like, or [`None`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/constants.html#None "(in Python v3.15)"), optional
        :   If *header* is not provided or None, the object will be
            initialized to default values.

        **fobj**[`HDUList`](../io/fits/api/hdulists.html#astropy.io.fits.HDUList "astropy.io.fits.HDUList"), optional
        :   It is needed when header keywords point to a [distortion
            paper](https://www.atnf.csiro.au/people/mcalabre/WCS/dcs_20040422.pdf) lookup table stored in a different extension.

        **key**[`str`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/stdtypes.html#str "(in Python v3.15)"), optional
        :   The name of a particular WCS transform to use. This may be
            either `' '` or `'A'`-`'Z'` and corresponds to the
            `"a"` part of the `CTYPEia` cards. *key* may only be
            provided if *header* is also provided.

        **minerr**[`float`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.15)"), optional
        :   The minimum value a distortion correction must have in order
            to be applied. If the value of `CQERRja` is smaller than
            *minerr*, the corresponding distortion is not applied.

        **relax**[bool](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/stdtypes.html#bltin-boolean-values "(in Python v3.15)") or [`int`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/functions.html#int "(in Python v3.15)"), optional
        :   Degree of permissiveness:

            * [`True`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.15)") (default): Admit all recognized informal extensions
              of the WCS standard.
            * [`False`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/constants.html#False "(in Python v3.15)"): Recognize only FITS keywords defined by the
              published WCS standard.
            * [`int`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/functions.html#int "(in Python v3.15)"): a bit field selecting specific extensions to accept.
              See [Header-reading relaxation constants](../wcs/relax.html#relaxread) for details.

        **naxis**[`int`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/functions.html#int "(in Python v3.15)") or [sequence](https://docs.python.org/3/glossary.html#term-sequence "(in Python v3.15)"), optional
        :   Extracts specific coordinate axes using
            [`sub()`](astropy.wcs.Wcsprm.html#astropy.wcs.Wcsprm.sub "astropy.wcs.Wcsprm.sub"). If a header is provided, and
            *naxis* is not `None`, *naxis* will be passed to
            [`sub()`](astropy.wcs.Wcsprm.html#astropy.wcs.Wcsprm.sub "astropy.wcs.Wcsprm.sub") in order to select specific
            axes from the header. See [`sub()`](astropy.wcs.Wcsprm.html#astropy.wcs.Wcsprm.sub "astropy.wcs.Wcsprm.sub") for
            more details about this parameter.

        **keysel**[sequence](https://docs.python.org/3/glossary.html#term-sequence "(in Python v3.15)") of [`str`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/stdtypes.html#str "(in Python v3.15)"), optional
        :   A sequence of flags used to select the keyword types
            considered by wcslib. When `None`, only the standard image
            header keywords are considered (and the underlying wcspih() C
            function is called). To use binary table image array or pixel
            list keywords, *keysel* must be set.

            Each element in the list should be one of the following
            strings:

            * ‘image’: Image header keywords
            * ‘binary’: Binary table image array keywords
            * ‘pixel’: Pixel list keywords

            Keywords such as `EQUIna` or `RFRQna` that are common to
            binary table image arrays and pixel lists (including
            `WCSNna` and `TWCSna`) are selected by both ‘binary’ and
            ‘pixel’.

        **colsel**[sequence](https://docs.python.org/3/glossary.html#term-sequence "(in Python v3.15)") of [`int`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/functions.html#int "(in Python v3.15)"), optional
        :   A sequence of table column numbers used to restrict the WCS
            transformations considered to only those pertaining to the
            specified columns. If [`None`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/constants.html#None "(in Python v3.15)"), there is no restriction.

        **fix**[bool](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/stdtypes.html#bltin-boolean-values "(in Python v3.15)"), optional
        :   When [`True`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.15)") (default), call [`fix`](astropy.wcs.Wcsprm.html#astropy.wcs.Wcsprm.fix "astropy.wcs.Wcsprm.fix") on
            the resulting object to fix any non-standard uses in the
            header. [`FITSFixedWarning`](astropy.wcs.FITSFixedWarning.html#astropy.wcs.FITSFixedWarning "astropy.wcs.FITSFixedWarning") Warnings will be emitted if any
            changes were made.

        **translate\_units**[`str`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/stdtypes.html#str "(in Python v3.15)"), optional
        :   Specify which potentially unsafe translations of non-standard
            unit strings to perform. By default, performs none. See
            [`WCS.fix`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.fix "astropy.wcs.WCS.fix") for more information about this parameter. Only
            effective when `fix` is [`True`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.15)").

        **preserve\_units**[bool](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/stdtypes.html#bltin-boolean-values "(in Python v3.15)"), optional
        :   By default, some units are converted to SI, for example spectral
            axes in units of nm might be converted to m, and celestial axes
            in units of arcsec might be converted to deg. If `preserve_units`
            is set to [`True`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.15)"), the original units will be preserved.

    Raises:
    :   [`MemoryError`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/exceptions.html#MemoryError "(in Python v3.15)")
        :   Memory allocation failed.

        [`ValueError`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/exceptions.html#ValueError "(in Python v3.15)")
        :   Invalid key.

        [`KeyError`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/exceptions.html#KeyError "(in Python v3.15)")
        :   Key not found in FITS header.

        [`ValueError`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/exceptions.html#ValueError "(in Python v3.15)")
        :   Lookup table distortion present in the header but *fobj* was
            not provided.

    Notes

    1. astropy.wcs supports arbitrary *n* dimensions for the core WCS
       (the transformations handled by WCSLIB). However, the
       [distortion paper](https://www.atnf.csiro.au/people/mcalabre/WCS/dcs_20040422.pdf) lookup table and [SIP](https://irsa.ipac.caltech.edu/data/SPITZER/docs/files/spitzer/shupeADASS.pdf) distortions must be
       two dimensional. Therefore, if you try to create a WCS object
       where the core WCS has a different number of dimensions than 2
       and that object also contains a [distortion paper](https://www.atnf.csiro.au/people/mcalabre/WCS/dcs_20040422.pdf) lookup
       table or [SIP](https://irsa.ipac.caltech.edu/data/SPITZER/docs/files/spitzer/shupeADASS.pdf) distortion, a [`ValueError`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/exceptions.html#ValueError "(in Python v3.15)")
       exception will be raised. To avoid this, consider using the
       *naxis* kwarg to select two dimensions from the core WCS.
    2. The number of coordinate axes in the transformation is not
       determined directly from the `NAXIS` keyword but instead from
       the highest of:

       > * `NAXIS` keyword
       > * `WCSAXESa` keyword
       > * The highest axis number in any parameterized WCS keyword.
       >   The keyvalue, as well as the keyword, must be
       >   syntactically valid otherwise it will not be considered.

       If none of these keyword types is present, i.e. if the header
       only contains auxiliary WCS keywords for a particular
       coordinate representation, then no coordinate description is
       constructed for it.

       The number of axes, which is set as the `naxis` member, may
       differ for different coordinate representations of the same
       image.
    3. When the header includes duplicate keywords, in most cases the
       last encountered is used.
    4. [`set`](astropy.wcs.Wcsprm.html#astropy.wcs.Wcsprm.set "astropy.wcs.Wcsprm.set") is called immediately after
       construction, so any invalid keywords or transformations will
       be raised by the constructor, not when subsequently calling a
       transformation method.

    Attributes Summary

    |  |  |
    | --- | --- |
    | [`array_shape`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.array_shape "astropy.wcs.WCS.array_shape") | The shape of the data that the WCS applies to as a tuple of length [`pixel_n_dim`](astropy.wcs.wcsapi.BaseLowLevelWCS.html#astropy.wcs.wcsapi.BaseLowLevelWCS.pixel_n_dim "astropy.wcs.wcsapi.BaseLowLevelWCS.pixel_n_dim") in `(row, column)` order (the convention for arrays in Python). |
    | [`axis_correlation_matrix`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.axis_correlation_matrix "astropy.wcs.WCS.axis_correlation_matrix") | Returns an ([`world_n_dim`](astropy.wcs.wcsapi.BaseLowLevelWCS.html#astropy.wcs.wcsapi.BaseLowLevelWCS.world_n_dim "astropy.wcs.wcsapi.BaseLowLevelWCS.world_n_dim"), [`pixel_n_dim`](astropy.wcs.wcsapi.BaseLowLevelWCS.html#astropy.wcs.wcsapi.BaseLowLevelWCS.pixel_n_dim "astropy.wcs.wcsapi.BaseLowLevelWCS.pixel_n_dim")) matrix that indicates using booleans whether a given world coordinate depends on a given pixel coordinate. |
    | [`axis_type_names`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.axis_type_names "astropy.wcs.WCS.axis_type_names") | World names for each coordinate axis. |
    | [`celestial`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.celestial "astropy.wcs.WCS.celestial") | A copy of the current WCS with only the celestial axes included. |
    | [`cpdis1`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.cpdis1 "astropy.wcs.WCS.cpdis1") | [`DistortionLookupTable`](astropy.wcs.DistortionLookupTable.html#astropy.wcs.DistortionLookupTable "astropy.wcs.DistortionLookupTable") |
    | [`cpdis2`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.cpdis2 "astropy.wcs.WCS.cpdis2") | [`DistortionLookupTable`](astropy.wcs.DistortionLookupTable.html#astropy.wcs.DistortionLookupTable "astropy.wcs.DistortionLookupTable") |
    | [`det2im1`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.det2im1 "astropy.wcs.WCS.det2im1") | A [`DistortionLookupTable`](astropy.wcs.DistortionLookupTable.html#astropy.wcs.DistortionLookupTable "astropy.wcs.DistortionLookupTable") object for detector to image plane correction in the *x*-axis. |
    | [`det2im2`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.det2im2 "astropy.wcs.WCS.det2im2") | A [`DistortionLookupTable`](astropy.wcs.DistortionLookupTable.html#astropy.wcs.DistortionLookupTable "astropy.wcs.DistortionLookupTable") object for detector to image plane correction in the *y*-axis. |
    | [`has_celestial`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.has_celestial "astropy.wcs.WCS.has_celestial") |  |
    | [`has_distortion`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.has_distortion "astropy.wcs.WCS.has_distortion") | Returns [`True`](https://docs.python.org/3/builtins/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.15)") if any distortion terms are present. |
    | [`has_spectral`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.has_spectral "astropy.wcs.WCS.has_spectral") |  |
    | [`has_temporal`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.has_temporal "astropy.wcs.WCS.has_temporal") |  |
    | [`is_celestial`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.is_celestial "astropy.wcs.WCS.is_celestial") |  |
    | [`is_spectral`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.is_spectral "astropy.wcs.WCS.is_spectral") |  |
    | [`is_temporal`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.is_temporal "astropy.wcs.WCS.is_temporal") |  |
    | [`low_level_wcs`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.low_level_wcs "astropy.wcs.WCS.low_level_wcs") | Returns a reference to the underlying low-level WCS object. |
    | [`pixel_axis_names`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.pixel_axis_names "astropy.wcs.WCS.pixel_axis_names") | An iterable of strings describing the name for each pixel axis. |
    | [`pixel_bounds`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.pixel_bounds "astropy.wcs.WCS.pixel_bounds") | The bounds (in pixel coordinates) inside which the WCS is defined, as a list with [`pixel_n_dim`](astropy.wcs.wcsapi.BaseLowLevelWCS.html#astropy.wcs.wcsapi.BaseLowLevelWCS.pixel_n_dim "astropy.wcs.wcsapi.BaseLowLevelWCS.pixel_n_dim") `(min, max)` tuples. |
    | [`pixel_n_dim`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.pixel_n_dim "astropy.wcs.WCS.pixel_n_dim") | The number of axes in the pixel coordinate system. |
    | [`pixel_scale_matrix`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.pixel_scale_matrix "astropy.wcs.WCS.pixel_scale_matrix") |  |
    | [`pixel_shape`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.pixel_shape "astropy.wcs.WCS.pixel_shape") | The shape of the data that the WCS applies to as a tuple of length [`pixel_n_dim`](astropy.wcs.wcsapi.BaseLowLevelWCS.html#astropy.wcs.wcsapi.BaseLowLevelWCS.pixel_n_dim "astropy.wcs.wcsapi.BaseLowLevelWCS.pixel_n_dim") in `(x, y)` order (where for an image, `x` is the horizontal coordinate and `y` is the vertical coordinate). |
    | [`preserve_units`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.preserve_units "astropy.wcs.WCS.preserve_units") | Indicates whether the `WCS` class is preserving the original units. |
    | [`serialized_classes`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.serialized_classes "astropy.wcs.WCS.serialized_classes") | Indicates whether Python objects are given in serialized form or as actual Python objects. |
    | [`sip`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.sip "astropy.wcs.WCS.sip") | Get/set the [`Sip`](astropy.wcs.Sip.html#astropy.wcs.Sip "astropy.wcs.Sip") object for performing [SIP](https://irsa.ipac.caltech.edu/data/SPITZER/docs/files/spitzer/shupeADASS.pdf) distortion correction. |
    | [`spectral`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.spectral "astropy.wcs.WCS.spectral") | A copy of the current WCS with only the spectral axes included. |
    | [`temporal`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.temporal "astropy.wcs.WCS.temporal") | A copy of the current WCS with only the time axes included. |
    | [`wcs`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.wcs "astropy.wcs.WCS.wcs") | A [`Wcsprm`](astropy.wcs.Wcsprm.html#astropy.wcs.Wcsprm "astropy.wcs.Wcsprm") object to perform the basic [wcslib](https://www.atnf.csiro.au/people/mcalabre/WCS/wcslib/index.html) WCS transformation. |
    | [`world_axis_names`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.world_axis_names "astropy.wcs.WCS.world_axis_names") | An iterable of strings describing the name for each world axis. |
    | [`world_axis_object_classes`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.world_axis_object_classes "astropy.wcs.WCS.world_axis_object_classes") | A dictionary giving information on constructing high-level objects for the world coordinates. |
    | [`world_axis_object_components`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.world_axis_object_components "astropy.wcs.WCS.world_axis_object_components") | A list with [`world_n_dim`](astropy.wcs.wcsapi.BaseLowLevelWCS.html#astropy.wcs.wcsapi.BaseLowLevelWCS.world_n_dim "astropy.wcs.wcsapi.BaseLowLevelWCS.world_n_dim") elements giving information on constructing high-level objects for the world coordinates. |
    | [`world_axis_physical_types`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.world_axis_physical_types "astropy.wcs.WCS.world_axis_physical_types") | An iterable of strings describing the physical type for each world axis. |
    | [`world_axis_units`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.world_axis_units "astropy.wcs.WCS.world_axis_units") | An iterable of strings given the units of the world coordinates for each axis. |
    | [`world_n_dim`](#astropy.wcs.WCS.world_n_dim "astropy.wcs.WCS.world_n_dim") | The number of axes in the world coordinate system. |
